Q({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\to\mathscr{B}
. The universal property of
Q({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
as formulated in
Lemma

A.5
implies that a
2
-functor
Q({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\to\mathscr{B}
is specified by a choice of
0,1,2
-morphisms and relations as above.
∎

Now that we have a useful description of pseudofunctors out of
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
, the next step is to study pseudofunctorial lifts to
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
. Recall that the category
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
is the join of
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
and a final object (
3.12
). To characterize pseudofunctors out of
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
directly is challenging, so we prefer instead to study
2
-functors out of its cofibrant replacement, which requires proving results analogous to
Lemma

A.5
and
Lemma

A.10
. First we describe
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
in terms generators and relations, analogously to
Lemma

A.2
.

Lemma A.11
.

Let
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
be the category constructed by joining a terminal object to
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
. More precisely,
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
is specified by following generators and relations.

•

Objects
0,1,2,f

•

Morphisms

–

The generating morphisms in
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
:
s_{0}:0\to 1
,
d_{0},d_{1}:1\to 0
,
s_{0},s_{1}:1\to 2
,
d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}:2\to 1
,

–

Three additional morphisms:
t_{0}:0\to f
,
t_{1}:1\to f
,
t_{2}:2\to f
.

•

Relations

–

The relations that already hold in
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
:

*

d_{0}d_{0}=d_{0}d_{1}
,
d_{0}d_{2}=d_{1}d_{0}
,
d_{1}d_{1}=d_{1}d_{2}:2\to 0
,

*

d_{1}s_{0}=\mathrm{id}=d_{0}s_{0}:0\to 0
,

*

d_{0}s_{0}=d_{1}s_{1}=\mathrm{id}=d_{1}s_{0}=d_{2}s_{1}:1\to 1
,

*

s_{0}s_{0}=s_{1}s_{0}:0\to 2
,

–

The additional relations for the terminal object:

*

t_{0}=t_{1}s_{0}=t_{2}s_{0}s_{0}=t_{2}s_{1}s_{0}:0\to f
,

*

t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{1}=t_{2}s_{0}=t_{2}s_{1}:1\to f
,

*

t_{2}=t_{1}d_{0}=t_{1}d_{1}=t_{1}d_{2}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{2}=t_{0}d_{1}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{1}d_{2}=t_{0}d_{1}d_{1}:2\to f
.

There is an obvious fully faithful inclusion
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\to(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
. The list of relations for
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
given above contains many redundancies, which we can reduce as follows.

Lemma A.12
.

The category
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
can be specified by the following data.

•

The same objects as above.

•

The generating morphisms in
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
together with one additional morphism
t_{0}:0\to f

•

The relations that hold in
({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}
together with one additional relation
t_{0}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{1}:1\to f
.

Proof.

We define
t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}
and
t_{2}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}
.
Since the relations listed in this lemma are a subset of the relations in
Lemma

A.11
, the necessity is evident. We prove that these relations suffice by recovering the remaining relations from them, as follows.

(1)

t_{2}s_{0}s_{0}\overset{s_{0}s_{0}=s_{1}s_{0}}{=}t_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
.

(2)

t_{2}\overset{def}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}\overset{t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{1}d_{0}

(3)

t_{2}\overset{def}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}\overset{d_{0}d_{0}=d_{0}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{1}\overset{t_{0}d_{0}=t_{1}}{=}t_{1}d_{1}

(4)

t_{2}\overset{def}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}\overset{t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{1}d_{0}\overset{t_{1}=t_{0}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{1}d_{0}\overset{d_{1}d_{0}=d_{0}d_{2}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{2}\overset{t_{0}d_{0}=t_{1}}{=}t_{1}d_{2}

(5)

t_{2}\overset{def}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}\overset{t_{0}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{1}d_{0}\overset{d_{1}d_{0}=d_{0}d_{2}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{2}\overset{t_{0}d_{0}=t_{0}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{1}d_{2}\overset{d_{1}d_{2}=d_{1}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{1}d_{1}

(6)

t_{2}s_{0}\overset{t_{2}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{1}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{d_{0}s_{0}=\mathrm{id}}{=}t_{1}

(7)

t_{2}s_{1}\overset{t_{2}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}s_{1}\overset{d_{0}d_{0}=d_{0}d_{1}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{1}s_{1}\overset{t_{0}d_{0}=t_{1}}{=}t_{1}d_{1}s_{1}\overset{d_{1}s_{1}=\mathrm{id}}{=}t_{1}

(8)

t_{2}s_{0}s_{0}\overset{t_{2}=t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}s_{0}s_{0}\overset{\mathrm{id}=d_{0}s_{0}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{\mathrm{id}=d_{0}s_{0}}{=}t_{0}

(9)

t_{1}s_{0}\overset{t_{1}=t_{0}d_{0}}{=}t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{d_{0}s_{0}=\mathrm{id}}{=}t_{0}

As we have recovered all the equalities in
Lemma

A.11
, the result follows.
∎

Given this lemma, we can easily modify the proofs of
Lemma

A.5
and
Lemma

A.10
to establish the following analogous result.

Definition A.13
.

Let
(\mathcal{D}_{2})^{\rhd}
denote the graph with

•

vertices
0,1,2,f
, and

•

the same edges as in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
(
A.3
), together with one additional edge
0\to f
.

Lemma A.14
.

The
2
-category
\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}
admits a cofibrant replacement given by the
2
-category
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)
, which is the
2
-category determined by the computad with

•

underlying graph is
(\mathcal{D}_{2})^{\rhd}
, and

•

the same
2
-arrows as
\mathcal{G}_{2}
(
A.4
), along with one additional
2
-arrow
\theta:t_{0}d_{0}\Rightarrow t_{0}d_{1}
.

The morphism categories of
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)
can be characterized as in
Lemma

A.8
, leading in particular to the following observation.

Corollary A.15
.

The category
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(i,f)
is a contractible groupoid for all
i\in\{0,1,2\}
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as that of
Lemma

A.8
, given that there are unique maps
i\to f
in
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)
.
∎

Remark A.16
.

A.15
enables us to better understand
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)
. There are four objects
0,1,2,f
, and morphisms are words with letters

s_{0},d_{0},d_{1},s_{0},s_{1},d_{0},d_{1},d_{2},t_{0}.

In particular, the only generating morphism that has codomain
f
is
t_{0}:0\to f
, whence every object in the groupoid
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(i,f)
is necessarily of the form
t_{0}g
for some morphism
g:i\to 0
, where
i\in\{0,1,2\}
. Contractibility of
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(i,f)
implies that there is precisely one isomorphism between
t_{0}g_{0}
and
t_{0}g_{1}
for any
g_{0},g_{1}:i\to 0
.

The equivalence below of
2
-functors and pseudofunctors follows from
Lemma

A.14
and
[
Lac02
, Remark 4.10]
.

Corollary A.17
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
be a
2
-category.
Precomposing with the cofibrant replacement functor

\pi_{Q}^{\rhd}:Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)\to\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}

induces an equivalence between the category of pseudofunctors
\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{B}
and that of
2
-functors
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)\to\mathscr{B}
. In other words, for every pseudofunctor
F:\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{B}
, there exists a
2
-functor
\widehat{F}:Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)\to\mathscr{B}
and an equivalence
F\circ\pi_{Q}^{\rhd}\simeq\widehat{F}
.

We can use this result to characterize pseudofunctors out of
(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
, using the inclusion functor
Q({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\to Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)
arising from the inclusion of computads. In the statement and the proof below, we use the notation of
Lemma

A.10
.

Lemma A.18
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
be a
2
-category, and let
F:({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\to\mathscr{B}
be a pseudofunctor. An extension of
F
to
\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}
is specified by
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}}

•

an object
B_{f}
in
\mathscr{B}
,

•

a
1
-morphism
t_{0}:B_{0}\to B_{f}
, and

•

an invertible
2
-morphism
t_{0}d_{0}\overset{\theta}{\Rightarrow}t_{0}d_{1}

such that the following equalities hold, where
*
denotes whiskering of 2-cells by 1-cells, and
\circ
denotes vertical composition of 2-cells.

•

\theta*s_{0}=t_{0}*((\beta_{0})^{-1}\circ\beta_{1}):t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\to t_{0}d_{1}s_{0}

•

(t_{0}(\alpha_{1})^{-1})\circ(\theta*d_{0})=(\theta^{-1}*d_{2})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{2})\circ(\theta*d_{1})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{0}):t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}\to t_{0}d_{0}d_{2}
.

Proof.

By
A.9
and
A.17
, we can instead start with a
2
-functor
F:Q(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})\to\mathscr{B}
and define a lift
\widehat{F}:Q(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}\to\mathscr{B}
. The nature of the data required to specify the image of
0,1
,and
2
-morphisms under
\widehat{F}
follows from the characterization of
Q(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op})^{\rhd}
in
Lemma

A.14
.

In order to complete the proof we make explicit the relations that guarantee that the groupoids
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(i,f)
are in fact contractible, i.e., that for any two morphisms
g_{1},g_{2}:i\to f
, there is a
unique
natural isomorphism
g_{1}\cong g_{2}
. Any other natural isomorphism generated by
\theta:t_{0}d_{0}\cong t_{0}d_{1}
must therefore coincide with the original one.

We start by analyzing
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(1,f)
. As explained in
A.16
, every object in this category is of the form
t_{0}g^{\prime}
for some
g^{\prime}:1\to 0
. By construction, every object
g^{\prime}
in
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(1,0)
is
uniquely
isomorphic to either
d_{0}:1\to 0
or
d_{1}:1\to 0
. For any two 1-cells
g_{1},g_{2}:1\to t
, with
g_{1}=t_{0}g_{1}^{\prime}
,
g_{2}=t_{0}g_{2}^{\prime}
, there are thus four possible scenarios.

(1)

There are unique isomorphisms
g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{1}}{\cong}d_{0}
and
g_{2}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{2}}{\cong}d_{0}
, whence

g_{1}=t_{0}g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{t_{0}\kappa_{1}}{\cong}t_{0}d_{0}\overset{t_{0}\kappa_{2}}{\cong}t_{0}g_{2}^{\prime}=g_{2}

is the unique isomorphism between
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
.

(2)

There are unique isomorphisms
g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{1}}{\cong}d_{1}
and
g_{2}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{2}}{\cong}d_{1}
. This case is essentially identical to the previous one.

(3)

There are unique isomorphisms
g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{1}}{\cong}d_{0}
and
g_{2}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{2}}{\cong}d_{1}
. In this case the unique isomorphism between
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
is obtained as follows.

g_{1}=t_{0}g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{t_{0}\kappa_{1}}{\cong}t_{0}d_{0}\overset{\theta}{\cong}t_{0}d_{1}\overset{t_{0}\kappa_{2}}{\cong}t_{0}g_{2}^{\prime}=g_{2}

(4)

There are unique isomorphisms
g_{1}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{1}}{\cong}d_{1}
and
g_{2}^{\prime}\overset{\kappa_{2}}{\cong}d_{0}
. This case is the same as the previous one, up to permutation of the roles of
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
.

Consider now the case of
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(0,f)
.
There are three types of objects in this category.

(1)

t_{0}g
, where
g:0\to 0
is uniquely isomorphic to the identity in
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(0,0)
.

(2)

t_{0}d_{0}g
, where
g:0\to 1
is uniquely isomorphic to
s_{0}
in
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(0,1)
.

(3)

t_{0}d_{1}g
, where
g:0\to 1
is uniquely isomorphic to
s_{0}
in
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(0,1)
.

As in the previous case, there is a unique isomorphism
t_{0}d_{0}g\cong t_{0}d_{1}g
. We need therefore only to compare a 1-cell of the form
t_{0}g_{1}
, where
g_{1}\cong\mathrm{id}_{0}
, and a 1-cell of the form
t_{0}d_{0}g_{2}
, where
g_{2}\cong s_{0}
, and to find the necessary and sufficient conditions to guarantee that there is a unique isomorphism between
t_{0}g_{1}
and
t_{0}d_{0}g_{2}
.

By assumption,
t_{0}g_{1}\cong t_{0}
and
t_{0}d_{0}g_{2}\cong t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}
, via unique isomorphisms. There are two potential isomorphisms between
t_{0}
and
t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}
:

(1)

t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{t_{0}*\beta_{1}}{\Rightarrow}t_{0}
, and

(2)

t_{0}d_{0}s_{0}\overset{\theta*s_{0}}{\Rightarrow}t_{0}d_{1}s_{0}\overset{t_{0}*\beta_{0}}{\Rightarrow}t_{0}
.

Hence, there is a unique isomorphism
t_{0}g_{1}\cong t_{0}d_{0}g_{2}
if and only if
\theta*s_{0}=t_{0}*((\beta_{0})^{-1}\circ\beta_{1})
.

The final case is that of
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(2,f)
. If
g:2\to f
is any 1-cell, then
g=t_{0}g^{\prime}
, for some
g^{\prime}:2\to 0
, which is necessarily of one of the following three types.

(1)

g^{\prime}\cong d_{0}d_{0}
via unique isomorphism

(2)

g^{\prime}\cong d_{0}d_{2}
via unique isomorphism

(3)

g^{\prime}\cong d_{1}d_{2}
via unique isomorphism

It suffices therefore to find the necessary and sufficient conditions such that for a choice of
g_{1}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}
among the three types above, there is a unique isomorphism
t_{0}g_{1}^{\prime}\cong t_{0}g_{2}^{\prime}
. There are three types of pairs of 1-cells to consider.

(1)

In the case where
g_{1}^{\prime}\cong g_{2}^{\prime}\cong d_{0}d_{0}
, observe that the diagram of unique isomorphisms between 1-cells
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{0}d_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{1}d_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{0}d_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{0}d_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{1}d_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}d_{1}d_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta*d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}*\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}*(\alpha_{1})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta*d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}*\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{-1}*d_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

.

commutes by uniqueness of isomorphisms between 1-cells. It follows that there is a unique isomorphism
t_{0}g_{1}^{\prime}\cong t_{0}g_{2}^{\prime}
if and only if

(t_{0}(\alpha_{1})^{-1})\circ(\theta*d_{0})=(\theta^{-1}*d_{2})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{2})\circ(\theta*d_{1})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{0})

(2)

When
g_{1}^{\prime}\cong d_{0}d_{0},g_{2}^{\prime}\cong d_{0}d_{2}
, the same commuting diagram as in the previous case implies that the same necessary and sufficient conditions apply in this case.

(3)

When
g_{1}^{\prime}=d_{0}d_{2}
,
g_{2}^{\prime}=d_{1}d_{2}
, the same commuting diagram again leads to the same necessary and sufficient conditions.

It follows that
Q\Big(\big(({\mathbbe{\Delta}}_{\leq 2})^{op}\big)^{\rhd}\Big)(i,f)
is contractible for
i=0,1,2
if and only if

\displaystyle\theta*s_{0}

\displaystyle=t_{0}*((\beta_{0})^{-1}\circ\beta_{1})

\displaystyle(t_{0}(\alpha_{1})^{-1})\circ(\theta*d_{0})

\displaystyle=(\theta^{-1}*d_{2})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{2})\circ(\theta*d_{1})\circ(t_{0}*\alpha_{0}),

which finishes the proof.
∎

Remark A.19
.

The results of this section (and in particular
Lemma

A.18
) focused on the case where
\mathscr{B}
is a
2
-category, as we apply these results only to the
2
-category of categories (in
3.19
). However, following
[
Lac04
, Lemma 9]
, an
analogous result should certainly hold for bicategories and pseudofunctors of bicategories (called
homomorphisms of bicategories
in
[
Lac04
]
).
